Such conduct involves wrongdoing and aggression against neighbours, in addition to the prohibited pastime itself. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 15849

Chapter on Social Relations

Shaykh al-Islām—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked:

About people who keep company with “beardless youths.” One of them may kiss a boy or lie beside him, yet they claim that they keep such company for Allah’s sake and regard it as neither sinful nor shameful. They say, “We keep company with them without indecency.” The boy’s father, paternal uncle and brother know about this but do not object. What is Allah Most High’s ruling concerning these people? How should a Muslim deal with them under these circumstances?

The Answer

He replied:

Praise be to Allah. A handsome, beardless boy is, in many respects, subject to the same rules as a non-mahram woman. It is not permissible to kiss him for sensual pleasure. Rather, only someone who can be trusted with him, such as his father or brothers, may kiss him. Looking at him with such pleasure is likewise impermissible by unanimous agreement. Indeed, the majority hold that looking at him is forbidden when there is a fear of such pleasure arising. One may look at him only when there is a need and no improper desire, such as when conducting transactions with him, bearing witness concerning him, or similar circumstances—just as one may look at a woman when there is a need.

As for “lying beside him,” this is far too indecent for its ruling even to need asking about. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Instruct them to pray at seven; strike them for neglecting it at ten; and separate

---

## Page 15850

them in their beds”—when they have reached ten years of age and have not yet experienced a wet dream. What, then, of those older than that? And if the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“No man is alone with a woman without Satan being the third among them,”

and also said:

“Beware of entering upon women.” They asked, “O Messenger of Allah, what about a husband’s male relative?” He replied, “The husband’s male relative is death.”

If being alone together is forbidden because of what is feared may result from it, what, then, of lying together?

As for someone’s claim that he does this for Allah’s sake, such claims are mostly lies. He may indeed have an intention to act for Allah’s sake, but mixed with his own desires—just as some make similar claims about keeping company with non-mahram women. The matter then remains as Allah Most High said concerning wine:

“In them both is great sin and benefit for people, but their sin is greater than their benefit.”

Al-Sha‘bī related from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“When the delegation of ‘Abd al-Qays came to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, there was among them a strikingly handsome boy. He seated him behind his back and said, ‘David’s sin, peace be upon him, was only a look.’”

This was despite his being the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and being married to nine women. The delegation consisted of righteous people, and such indecency was not known among the Arabs. So many warnings against keeping company with “young lads” have been transmitted from the shaykhs that recounting them would take a long time.

No one may do anything that leads to these forbidden evils, even if he combines it with something beneficial, such as teaching or moral training. “Beardless youths” can be taught and trained without these evils, which harm them, their companions, and the Muslims—through ill suspicions at times and doubts at others. Indeed, it is related that there was a man with whom would sit

---

## Page 15851

beardless youths. ‘Umar, may Allah be pleased with him, forbade people to sit with him. ‘Umar ibn al-Khattāb also encountered a young man and cut his hair because some women were attracted to him, besides banishing him from his homeland and separating him from his family.

Anyone who allows a boy under his care—such as his son, brother, slave, or an orphan—to remain with someone who associates with him in this manner is a cursed man who condones such indecency:

“No one who condones such indecency will enter Paradise.”

For hidden indecency is not ordinarily established by proof; proof is established only for what is outward, and this vile companionship is itself a form of outward indecency. Allah Most High has said:

“Do not approach indecencies, whether openly or in secret.”

And He Most High has said:

“Say: My Lord has only forbidden indecencies, whether openly or in secret.”

Were we to recount the harm and corruption that have resulted from such conduct, together with what the scholars have said about it, the account would be lengthy. This applies whether the man is pious or wicked. The pious man suffers bitterly as he struggles against his desires and resists his own inclinations, and his devil and his own self often overcome him. He is like someone carrying a burden beyond his strength, which torments him or kills him. As for the wicked man, this brings his wickedness to its fullest extent. Allah knows best.

He—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked:

“What is the ruling concerning two men who wagered over composing two zajal poems, each having his own partisan following, and concerning those who take sides with them? Their verse includes amorous descriptions of beardless youths and other subjects. What about others like these two? Give us your ruling, and may you be rewarded.”

---

## Page 15852

He replied:

All praise is due to Allah. Those who compete in composing these zajal poems and similar verse, together with the partisan supporters on either side—whether wagers are involved or not—are wrongdoers, transgressors and sinners. They deserve severe punishment under Islamic law to deter them and other foolish, misguided, disobedient evildoers like them from such words and deeds, which bring no benefit in religious or worldly affairs. Rather, they harm those who engage in them in both respects.

It is incumbent upon “those in authority and all Muslims” to denounce these people and their helpers until they abandon these reprehensible practices, return to obeying Allah and His Messenger, and adhere to the straight path that Muslims are obliged to follow. These contests involve reprehensible things, some forbidden and others not forbidden but disliked. Among the forbidden things involved are [those] (1) whose prohibition is established by consensus and by the scriptural texts. This can be explained in several respects.

“The first” is that wagering on this is forbidden by the consensus of the Muslims. Likewise, even if the money were provided by one of the two contestants or came from elsewhere, it would still be impermissible. This holds both according to those who maintain that prizes are permitted only for camel racing, horse racing or archery, and according to those who permit prizes in contests other than these three.

Under the first view, the reason is clear. On this subject, there is the well-known hadith recorded in the Sunan collections, in which the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“No prize is permitted except for camel racing, horse racing or archery.”

These three are activities undertaken in jihad in Allah’s cause, so providing prize money for them is a form of spending wealth in Allah’s cause. This differs from other permissible activities, such as wrestling and footracing, which are not activities of jihad. They have therefore been permitted without a prize. “For the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace,

> **Footnotes:**
> (1) The text in square brackets is absent from the printed edition, and I did not find it in Siyānat Majmū‘ al-Fatāwā min al-Saqt wa-l-Tashīf (Protecting the Collected Fatwas from Omissions and Textual Corruptions).
> Usāmah ibn al-Zahrā’ — book coordinator for al-Mawsū‘ah al-Shāmilah.

---

## Page 15853

wrestled Ibn ‘Abd Yazīd and raced ‘Ā’ishah, may Allah be pleased with her.” He also permitted Salamah ibn al-Akwa‘ to compete in a footrace. According to the second view, however, the contest must involve a permissible activity, which these contests do not. This becomes clear from “the second consideration”: these verses contain descriptions of beardless youths, expressions of passionate love for them, and preludes to sexual immorality with them that encourage such desires and inflame them in people’s hearts.

Anything that facilitates indecency or encourages it is forbidden. The prohibition of this is more emphatic than the prohibition of lamentation and wailing: those arouse grief, whereas this arouses wickedness. Grief may sometimes be permitted, but no wickedness is permitted. This is a form of “pandering.”

It is established in the Sahīh that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“A woman must not describe another woman to her husband so vividly that it is as though he were looking at her.”

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, thus forbade describing a woman lest her image take shape in the man’s mind. What, then, of someone who describes beardless youths in these terms and pursues indecency through such reprehensible words—words that unsettle a sound heart, blind a diseased heart, and drive a person towards painful punishment?

‘Umar, may Allah be pleased with him, ordered that a woman who wailed over the dead be beaten. She was beaten until her hair became exposed. He was asked, “Commander of the Faithful, her hair has become exposed!”

He replied, “She has no claim to inviolability. She urges people to give way to anguish, which Allah has forbidden, and discourages patience, which Allah has commanded. She stirs up the living and harms the dead. She sells her tears and weeps over another’s grief. She is not weeping over your deceased; she is only weeping to get your dirhams.”

‘Umar also learned that a young man named “Nasr

---

## Page 15854

ibn Hajjāj” had been celebrated in a woman’s song. He had the young man’s hair cut, but still found him handsome, so he banished him to Basra, saying, “No man whom women celebrate in song shall remain here with me.” How, then, would ‘Umar have reacted had he seen someone singing such metrical verses about beardless youths, amid widespread wickedness, openly committed indecencies, and so little enjoining of right and forbidding of wrong? Such people oppose Allah, His Messenger, and His religion. They invite people to what Allah has forbidden, turn them away from what Allah has commanded, and bar them from Allah’s path, seeking to make it crooked.

“The third consideration” is that this metrical speech is corrupt, both in its individual words and in its composition. They have altered and distorted the speech of the Arabs with expressions such as “mā‘ū, badaw, ‘adaw,” and others like them, which hearts and ears reject, and which repel sound minds and natural dispositions.

As for its “composition,” it does not conform to Arabic metres. It is neither poetry nor an example of any of its sixteen metres, nor does it belong to the forms of rhymed prose, epistles, or oratory. It is well known that “learning and teaching Arabic” is a communal obligation. The early generations used to discipline their children for grammatical errors. We are therefore commanded, either by way of obligation or recommendation, to preserve the rules of Arabic and correct speech that departs from them. This preserves our means of understanding the Book and the Sunnah and of following the Arabs in their manner of expression.

Even leaving people to persist in their grammatical errors would be a deficiency and a fault. What, then, of people who take sound Arabic speech and well-formed metres and corrupt them with such words and rhythms, spoiling the language and replacing pure Arabic with forms of gibberish babbled only by brazen, inarticulate foreigners?

---

## Page 15855

“The fourth consideration” is that contests of this kind cause enmity and hatred and turn people away from the remembrance of Allah and from prayer. They resemble cockfighting and clashes between rams, as well as the contests of ordinary people that harm them without benefiting them. Allah, glorified is He, has forbidden wine and games of chance—that is, gambling—because they turn people away from the remembrance of Allah and from prayer and cause enmity and hatred.

For something to constitute “prohibited gambling,” it need not involve a stake. Playing backgammon is forbidden by scholarly agreement even without a stake, notwithstanding an anomalous dissenting opinion that carries no weight. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Whoever plays backgammon has disobeyed Allah and His Messenger.”

This is because backgammon turns people away from the remembrance of Allah and from prayer and causes enmity and hatred. These contests turn people away from the remembrance of Allah and from prayer, and cause enmity and hatred among them, to an even greater degree than backgammon. If, then, most of the imams have prohibited chess, and Mālik considered it worse than backgammon—even though those who play backgammon and chess, sinful though they may be, are better than these people—the point is clear.

“The fifth consideration” is that most of these people are either hypocritical heretics or wicked, openly sinful people. A righteous believer is scarcely to be found among them. Indeed, skilled practitioners among them have been found to have cast off the religion of Islam, neglecting their prayers and following their desires. Such a person believes neither in Allah nor in the Last Day, does not regard as forbidden what Allah and His Messenger have forbidden, and does not follow the religion of the Muslims. If, however, he is a Muslim, he is an openly sinful person who commits forbidden acts and neglects obligations.

If what predominates among them is either hypocrisy or open sin, then Allah’s ruling concerning the heretic is that he be put to death without being asked to repent, while His ruling concerning the openly sinful person is that the prescribed punishment be carried out upon him, whether execution or otherwise. And one who associates

---

## Page 15856

with them and keeps their company will not have his claim to be free of such things accepted. For either he joins them in committing forbidden acts and neglecting obligations, or he condones their wrongdoing, neither enjoining what is right nor forbidding what is wrong.

In either case, he deserves punishment. A case involving people drinking wine was brought before ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz, who ordered that they receive the prescribed punishment of flogging. He was asked, “What about the man among them who is fasting?”

He replied, “Begin with the fasting man and flog him. Has he not heard the words of Allah Most High:

‘He has already revealed to you in the Book that when you hear Allah’s verses being denied and ridiculed, do not sit with them until they turn to another topic’?”

And His words, exalted is He:

“If Satan should cause you to forget, then once you remember, do not sit with the wrongdoing people.”

“Those who fear Allah are not accountable for them in any way, but must give a reminder, so that they may fear Him.”

He, glorified is He, thus forbade sitting with wrongdoers. What, then, of keeping their company? And what of taking them as close friends?

These people have abandoned gambling with their hands, being incapable of it, and have instead opened the door to gambling with their tongues. Gambling with the tongue is more destructive to reason and religion than gambling with the hands. Muslims must punish these people severely, shun them, and call upon them to repent. Indeed, even supposing a man composed these Arabic vernacular poems without exaggeration, he would be forbidden to do so—even if he composed them on subjects other than love.

For sometimes they compose them with expressions of disbelief in Allah, His Book, and His Messenger, as Abū al-Hasan al-Tustarī composed them on “the oneness of existence” and the claim that the Creator is the creation. At other times, they compose them on sinful themes, as do these wayward, foolish, openly sinful people.

Even supposing a poet composed these vernacular poems about the location of a shop, he would be forbidden to do so, for they corrupt the Arabic tongue and turn it into objectionable non-Arabic speech.

---

## Page 15857

The early generations consistently disapproved of changing the distinctive practices of the Arabs, even in everyday dealings—namely, “speaking languages other than Arabic”—unless there was a need, as Mālik, al-Shāfiʿī and Ahmad explicitly stated. Mālik even said, “Anyone who speaks a language other than Arabic in our mosque is to be removed from it.” This is despite its being permissible for speakers of other languages to speak them. They nevertheless allowed their use when needed and disapproved of it without need, in order to preserve the distinguishing features of Islam. For Allah revealed His Book in the Arabic tongue, sent His Arab Prophet with it, and made the Arab nation the best of nations. Preserving their distinctive practices thus became part of fully preserving Islam.

What, then, of someone who presumes to alter Arabic speech—its individual words and its verse—changing and replacing it, departing from its rules and compelling people to abandon it? This is comparable to what certain misguided, ignorant shaykhs do when they target a rational man, befuddle him and make him effeminate. They oppose the Messenger, who was sent to rectify people’s minds and religion and to perfect humankind, and who prohibited everything that impairs reason, in all its forms. When these people approach someone of sound mind and corrupt his reason and understanding, they have opposed Allah and defied His judgment.

Those who alter and corrupt the Arabic tongue incur a share of this blame and punishment in proportion to what they open the way to. A person is commanded to cultivate soundness of mind and speech, and this helps bring faith to completion. Its opposite leads to discord, misguidance and loss. Allah knows best.

He—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked:

“Is it permissible for someone to entertain people with fabricated statements and stories, all of them lies?”

---

## Page 15858

He replied:

Anyone who tells fabricated stories to make people laugh, or for some other purpose, is disobeying Allah and His Messenger. Bahz ibn Hakīm narrated from his father, from his grandfather, that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Whoever speaks and lies to make people laugh—woe to him, woe to him, then woe to him!”

Ibn Masʿūd said: “Lying is not acceptable, whether in earnest or in jest. Nor should any of you promise his child something and then fail to fulfil it.”

If this also involves wrongdoing against a Muslim and harm to religion, it is even more strictly forbidden. In any case, whoever does this deserves a punishment prescribed by Islamic law that will deter him from it. Allah knows best.

Shaykh al-Islām—may Allah have mercy on him—said:

Section

Imitating animals in matters condemned by Islamic law—whether in their sounds, actions, or the like—is blameworthy and forbidden. Examples include barking like dogs, braying like donkeys, and similar conduct.

There are several reasons for this:

First, we established in Iqtidāʾ al-Sirāt al-Mustaqīm (Following the Straight Path) that the Lawgiver prohibits imitating human groups that are deficient—such as Bedouins, non-Arabs, the People of the Book, and others like them—in matters distinctive to them. We also explained that one of the reasons for this is

---

## Page 15859

that resemblance gives rise to similarity in character. We mentioned that those who spend much time with certain animals acquire some of their traits, as do dog handlers and camel herders. We also cited the scriptural texts condemning those who are rough and hard-hearted—camel herders—and praising those who tend sheep. What, then, of imitating the animals themselves in the traits for which they are condemned?

Indeed, by even stronger implication, this principle entails a general prohibition against imitating animals in anything distinctive to them, even if that particular trait is not itself blameworthy, because doing so leads to conduct that is itself blameworthy. It is well known that being a Bedouin or a non-Arab is better than being a dog, a donkey, or a pig. Thus, if imitating these categories of human beings in their distinctive characteristics is prohibited because it involves imitating something that entails deficiency and leads to it, then imitating animals in their distinctive characteristics is even more deserving of condemnation and prohibition.

Second reason

For a human being to be like animals is blameworthy. Allah Most High says:

“We have certainly created for Hell many of the jinn and mankind. They have hearts with which they do not understand, eyes with which they do not see, and ears with which they do not hear. They are like livestock; rather, they are further astray. It is they who are heedless.”

Third reason

Allah, glorified is He, only likens a human being to a dog, a donkey, or similar animals in order to condemn him, as in His words:

“So his likeness is that of a dog: if you drive it away, it pants; if you leave it alone, it pants. Such is the likeness of the people who deny Our signs. So relate these accounts, that they may reflect.”

---

## Page 15860

“Evil is the example of the people who denied Our signs, and it was themselves they were wronging.”

And Allah Most High says:

“The likeness of those who were entrusted with the Torah but then failed to uphold it is that of a donkey carrying books”—to the end of the verse.

If resemblance to these animals is mentioned only by way of condemnation, even when the person condemned does not intend to resemble them, then someone who deliberately imitates them is all the more deserving of condemnation. If he imitates them in the very thing condemned by the Lawgiver, he is blameworthy on two counts. If he imitates them in something not specifically condemned, he is blameworthy because such imitation entails falling into what is specifically condemned. This is supported by the following:

Fourth reason

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said in an authentic hadith:

“One who takes back his gift is like one who returns to his vomit; no evil likeness befits us.”

In this connection, it is related that al-Shāfiʿī and Ahmad debated this issue. Al-Shāfiʿī said to him, “A dog is not subject to religious obligations.” Ahmad replied, “No evil likeness befits us.”

This argument is contained in the hadith itself. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, mentioned this comparison only to show that a person who resembles a dog is blameworthy, even though the dog is not blameworthy for that conduct in terms of religious accountability. Hence, no evil likeness befits us.

By saying, “Evil is the example,” Allah, glorified is He, has made clear that being likened to a dog is an evil likeness, and a believer should be free of any evil likeness. If he bears an evil likeness to a dog, he is blameworthy to the extent of that evil likeness.

Fifth reason

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“The angels do not enter a house in which there is a dog.”

He also said:

“When you hear roosters crowing, ask Allah for His bounty, and when

---

## Page 15861

you hear donkeys braying, seek refuge in Allah from Satan, for they have seen a devil.” This indicates that their sounds are accompanied by devils and drive the angels away. It is well known that whoever resembles something necessarily shares in its associated effects to the extent of that resemblance. Thus, if someone barks as they do, this brings a corresponding degree of association with devils and drives the angels away.

Whatever attracts devils and drives the angels away is permitted only out of necessity. Hence, keeping a dog is permitted only out of necessity: to obtain a benefit, such as hunting, or to ward off harm to livestock and crops. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, even said:

“Whoever keeps a dog, other than a dog for livestock, crops, or hunting, will have the reward for his deeds diminished by one qīrāt each day.”

In short, imitating something entails praise or blame according to the resemblance. Yet the fact that what is imitated is not subject to religious obligations does not exempt the imitator from those obligations, just as when someone imitates children or the insane. And Allah, glorified is He, knows best.

Sixth reason

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace,

“cursed men who imitate women and women who imitate men.”

This is because Allah created every kind of living creature and made its soundness and perfection depend both on features it shares with others and on features peculiar to it. Shared features are not distinctive to either kind, and thus are not subject to the prohibition; rather, the prohibition concerns distinctive features. Thus, if men are not permitted to imitate women in matters distinctive to women, and in matters distinctive to men it is not permissible

---

## Page 15862

for women to imitate them in those matters, then it is all the more impermissible for human beings to imitate beasts in matters distinctive to them.

This is because human beings and animals have both shared features and distinctive features that set them apart. Even shared activities—such as eating, drinking, sexual intercourse, making sounds, and moving—are accompanied by the distinctive human nature, so human beings are subject to particular rules concerning them. They may not imitate how animals perform these activities. This applies all the more to matters peculiar to animals. In reality, however, human beings and animals share no identical element; rather, human beings possess attributes that resemble those of animals in certain respects. The common element exists only in the mind, not in external reality.

Since this is so, Allah Most High has made human beings essentially different from animals and has placed their perfection and soundness in matters suited to them, in none of which are they identical to animals. Thus, if a person deliberately makes himself resemble an animal and alters Allah’s creation, he has fallen into conduct that corrupts the natural disposition and violates the revealed law. This is forbidden. And Allah knows best.

He—may Allah have mercy on him—also said:

Section

His words:

“So righteous women are devoutly obedient, guarding in their husbands’ absence what Allah would have them guard”

entail that a wife must obey her husband in all respects: serving him, travelling with him, granting him marital access, and so forth, as

---

## Page 15863

demonstrated by the Sunnah of the Messenger of Allah—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—in the hadith of “the red mountain,” the hadith concerning “prostration,” and others, just as obedience to one’s parents is obligatory. Every obligation to obey her parents has passed to her husband, and no obligation to obey her parents remains upon her. The former obligation arises from ties of kinship, and the latter from covenants, as we shall explain, Allah willing, when discussing these two great principles.

He—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked:

Concerning a woman who has married and is no longer under her parents’ authority: which is better for her—being dutiful to her parents or obeying her husband?

He answered:

Praise be to Allah, Lord of all worlds. When a woman marries, her husband has a greater claim over her than her parents, and her obligation to obey him is more binding. Allah Most High says:

“So righteous women are devoutly obedient, guarding in their husbands’ absence what Allah would have them guard.”

It is reported in a hadith that the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“This world is an enjoyment, and its best enjoyment is a righteous wife: when you look at her, she pleases you; when you command her, she obeys you; and when you are absent, she guards herself and your wealth for you.”

In Abū Hātim’s Sahīh, Abū Hurayrah reported that the Messenger of Allah—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“If a woman performs her five daily prayers, observes her month of fasting, guards her chastity, and obeys her husband, she will enter through whichever gate of Paradise she wishes.”

In al-Tirmidhī’s collection, Umm Salamah reported that the Messenger of Allah—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“Any woman who dies while her husband is pleased with her will enter

---

## Page 15864

Paradise.” Al-Tirmidhī said: “This is a good hadith.”

Abū Hurayrah reported that the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“If I were to command anyone to prostrate to another, I would command a woman to prostrate to her husband.”

Al-Tirmidhī narrated it and said: “This is a good hadith.” Abū Dāwūd also narrated it, with the wording:

“I would command women to prostrate to their husbands because of the rights Allah has given husbands over them.”

In the Musnad, Anas reported that the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“It is not fitting for one human being to prostrate to another. Were it fitting for one human being to prostrate to another, I would command a woman to prostrate to her husband, so great is his right over her. By Him in whose hand is my soul, if he had a sore extending from his foot to the top of his head, oozing pus and blood-stained fluid, and she came to him and licked him, she would still not have fulfilled his right.”

In the Musnad and Sunan Ibn Mājah, ‘Ā’ishah reported that the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“If I were to command anyone to prostrate to another, I would command a woman to prostrate to her husband. If a man commanded his wife to carry things from a red mountain to a black mountain, and from a black mountain to a red mountain, it would be proper for her to do so.”

That is, it would be her duty to do so.

Likewise, in the Musnad, Sunan Ibn Mājah, and Sahīh Ibn Hibbān, ‘Abd Allāh ibn Abī Awfā reported:

When Mu‘ādh returned from Syria, he prostrated to the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—who asked: “What is this, Mu‘ādh?”

He replied: “I went to Syria and found them prostrating to their bishops and dignitaries, so I wished that we would do the same for you, Messenger of Allah.”

The Messenger of Allah—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said: “Do not do that. If I were to command anyone to prostrate to anyone other than Allah, I would command

---

## Page 15865

a woman to prostrate to her husband. By Him in whose hand is Muhammad’s soul, a woman does not fulfil her Lord’s right until she fulfils her husband’s right. Even if he sought intimacy with her while she was seated on a camel saddle, she should not refuse him.”

Talq ibn ‘Alī reported that the Messenger of Allah—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“Whenever a man calls his wife to fulfil his need, she should come to him, even if she is tending the oven.”

Abū Hātim narrated it in his Sahīh, as did al-Tirmidhī, who said: “This is a good hadith.” In al-Sahīh, Abū Hurayrah reported that the Messenger of Allah—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“If a man calls his wife to his bed and she refuses to come, and he spends the night angry with her, the angels curse her until morning.”

There are many hadiths from the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—on this subject. Zayd ibn Thābit said: “The husband is called a master in Allah’s Book,” and recited His words, Exalted is He:

“And they found her master at the door.”

‘Umar ibn al-Khattāb said: “Marriage is bondage, so let each of you consider to whom he entrusts his cherished daughter in bondage.”

Al-Tirmidhī and others narrated that the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“Treat women well, for they are captives in your care.”

Thus, a woman’s position with her husband resembles that of a slave or captive. She may not leave his home without his permission, whether it is her father, her mother, or anyone else who tells her to do so. The imams agree on this.

If a man wishes to move elsewhere while fulfilling his obligations and observing Allah’s limits in his treatment of his wife, and her father forbids her to obey him in this, she must obey her husband rather than her parents. Both parents are acting wrongfully: they have no right to forbid her from obeying such a husband. Nor may she obey her mother if she tells her to seek release from the marriage or to harass him until he divorces her, such as demanding from him, by way of maintenance, clothing, and the dowry, what

---

## Page 15866

she demands in order to make him divorce her. She may not obey either parent in seeking divorce from him if he fears Allah in his treatment of her. In the four Sunan collections and the Sahīh of Ibn Abī Hātim, Thawbān reported that the Messenger of Allah—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“Any woman who asks her husband for divorce without any cause of distress will be forbidden the fragrance of Paradise.”

Another hadith states:

“Women who seek release from marriage and those who tear themselves away from their husbands are the hypocrites.”

However, if her parents, or either of them, instruct her to do something that constitutes obedience to Allah—such as observing the prayers, speaking truthfully, and fulfilling trusts—or forbid her to squander and waste her wealth, or anything similar that Allah and His Messenger have commanded or forbidden, she must comply. This applies even when the instruction comes from someone other than her parents; how much more so when it comes from her parents!

If her husband forbids her to do what Allah has commanded, or commands her to do what Allah has forbidden, she may not obey him in that, for the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“No created being may be obeyed in disobedience to the Creator.”

Indeed, even if a slave owner commands his slave to disobey Allah, the slave may not obey him in that disobedience. How, then, could a woman be permitted to obey her husband or either parent in disobedience to Allah? All good lies in obedience to Allah and His Messenger, and all evil lies in disobedience to Allah and His Messenger.

He—may Allah Most High have mercy on him—was asked:

“A man has a wife whom he has housed among vile people. He takes her out to entertainments and places of corruption, and associates with corrupt people. When he is told, ‘Move away from this evil dwelling,’ he replies, ‘I am her husband. I have authority over my wife, and the choice of residence is mine.’ Is he entitled to do this?”

---

## Page 15867

He replied:

Praise be to Allah, Lord of all worlds. He has no right to house her wherever he wishes or take her wherever he wishes. Rather, he must house her in accommodation suitable for a woman of her standing, and he must not take her into the company of immoral people. Indeed, he has no right to associate with immoral people in their wrongdoing. Whenever he does so, he must receive two punishments: one for his own wrongdoing, according to what he has done, and another for failing to safeguard his wife and taking her to places of immorality. He must be punished in a manner that deters him and others like him from such conduct. And Allah knows best.

He—may Allah Most High have mercy on him—also said:

Section

As for anal intercourse with women, this is forbidden according to the majority of the early and later scholars, as established in the Quran and Sunnah. This is also the well-known position in Mālik’s school.

As for the other view, which permits it, some transmit it as a position reported from Mālik, while others deny this attribution. Nāfiʿ reported from Ibn ʿUmar that when he recited to him:

“Your wives are a place of cultivation for you,”

Ibn ʿUmar told him, “It was revealed concerning anal intercourse with women.” Some say that Nāfiʿ misreported Ibn ʿUmar’s words or misunderstood his meaning: what Ibn ʿUmar meant was that it was revealed concerning vaginal intercourse with women from behind. For the scholars agree that the verse was revealed concerning this, and it used to be that

---

## Page 15868

the Jews forbade this and would say, “If a man has vaginal intercourse with a woman from behind, the child will be cross-eyed.” So Allah revealed this verse. The “place of cultivation” is the place where a child is conceived, namely, the vagina. Allah thus permitted a man to have vaginal intercourse with a woman from whichever direction he wished.

Sālim ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar used to say, “The slave has lied about my father.” This supports the view that Nāfiʿ misreported Ibn ʿUmar’s words, for they used the word “lying” to mean making a mistake. An example is ʿUbādah’s statement, “Abū Muhammad has lied,” when the latter said that the witr prayer was obligatory. Another is Ibn ʿAbbās’s statement, “Nawf has lied,” when Nawf said that the man who accompanied al-Khidr was not Moses of the Children of Israel.

Some say that Ibn ʿUmar himself misunderstood the verse. Allah knows best which of these occurred. However, Ibn ʿUmar is reported to have said, “Would a Muslim do this?” In any event, the meaning of the verse is what the Companions and Successors explained it to be, and the occasion of its revelation supports that interpretation. And Allah knows best.

He—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked:

A man has anal intercourse with his wife. Is this lawful or forbidden?

He replied:

Anal intercourse with a woman is forbidden according to the Quran and Sunnah. This is the position of the overwhelming majority of the early and later scholars. Indeed, it is the lesser form of sodomy. It is authentically established that the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“Allah is not shy of the truth. Do not have intercourse with women in

---

## Page 15869

their anuses.” Allah Most High has said:

“Your wives are a place of cultivation for you, so approach your place of cultivation however you wish.”

The “place of cultivation” is where a child is conceived, for cultivation takes place where things are planted and sown. The Jews used to say, “If a man approaches his wife from behind, the child will be cross-eyed.” Allah therefore revealed this verse and permitted a man to approach his wife from any direction, but only for vaginal intercourse. If he has anal intercourse with her and she willingly complies, both are to receive discretionary punishment. If they do not desist, they are to be separated, just as a man who engages in sexual immorality is separated from the person with whom he does so. And Allah knows best.

He—may Allah Most High have mercy on him—was asked:

What is required of someone who has anal intercourse with his wife? Has any scholar permitted it?

He replied:

All praise is due to Allah, Lord of all worlds. Anal intercourse is forbidden by the Book of Allah and the Sunnah of His Messenger—may Allah bless him and grant him peace. This is the position of the overwhelming majority of Muslim imams, including the Companions, the Successors, and others. Allah has said in His Book:

“Your wives are a place of cultivation for you, so approach your place of cultivation however you wish.”

It is established in the authentic hadith collection that the Jews used to say, “If a man has vaginal intercourse with his wife from behind, the child will be cross-eyed.” The Muslims asked the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—about this, whereupon Allah revealed this verse:

“Your wives are a place of cultivation for you, so approach your place of cultivation however you wish.”

A “place of cultivation” is where seeds are sown. A child is conceived only in the vagina, not in the anus. “So approach your place of cultivation” means the place where a child is conceived. “However you wish” means from whichever direction you wish: from in front of her, from behind her, from her right, or from her left. Allah Most High thus called women a place of cultivation and permitted intercourse only in those places of cultivation; and cultivation can only take place

---

## Page 15870

in the vagina.

A number of reports state that anal intercourse is the lesser form of sodomy. It is established that the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“Allah is not shy of the truth. Do not have intercourse with women in their anuses.”

The word hushsh means the anus, the place of filth. Allah, glorified be He, has forbidden intercourse with a menstruating woman, although the impurity in her vagina is temporary. What, then, of the place where gross impurity is found?

Moreover, this is a form of sodomy. Abū Hanīfah, the followers of al-Shāfi'ī, and Ahmad and his followers hold that it is forbidden, with no disagreement among them. This is also the apparent position of Mālik and his followers. Some people, however, have attributed a contrary position to them through another transmitted account. Others have rejected that account and challenged its authenticity. Its basis is a report attributed to Nāfi', which he related from Ibn 'Umar. Sālim ibn 'Abd Allāh used to deny Nāfi's account of this. Either Nāfi' made a mistake, or someone above him in the chain of transmission did.

Someone's mistake does not justify going against the Book and the Sunnah. Likewise, some people mistakenly permitted exchanging one dirham for two, whereas the imams agreed that this was forbidden because of the authentic hadiths concerning it. Similarly, some people were mistaken concerning certain kinds of drinks.

Since it is established that the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“Every intoxicant is wine, and all wine is forbidden,”

and that, when asked about various kinds of infused drinks, he said:

“Every intoxicant is forbidden,”

“Whatever intoxicates in large quantities is forbidden in small quantities,”

these established teachings of the Sunnah must be followed. There are comparable examples elsewhere in Islamic law.

If a man has anal intercourse with his wife, both must receive a punishment that deters them. If it is known that they will not desist, they must be separated. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 15871

Chapter on Dividing Time Between Wives

He—may Allah Most High have mercy on him—was asked:

What about a man married to two women who loves one more than the other, gives her more clothing and other provisions, and spends more time with her?

He replied:

Praise be to Allah. He must treat his two wives equitably, according to the consensus of the Muslims. In the four Sunan collections, Abū Hurayrah narrates that the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“Whoever has two wives and inclines towards one rather than the other will come on the Day of Resurrection with one side of his body leaning.”

He must therefore divide his time equally between them. If he spends one, two, or three nights with one, he must spend the same number with the other, without favouring either in the allocation of time. However, if he loves one more and has intercourse with her more often, there is no blame upon him for that. Concerning this, Allah Most High revealed:

“You will never be able to be equal between wives, even if you should strive to do so.”

That is, in love and sexual intercourse. In the four Sunan collections, ‘Ā’ishah said:

“The Messenger of Allah—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—would divide his time equitably and say, ‘This is my division concerning what I control, so do not blame me concerning what You control and I do not.’”

This refers to the heart.

---

## Page 15872

As for equitable treatment in “maintenance and clothing,” this too is the Sunnah, following the example of the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace. He treated his wives equitably in maintenance, just as he divided his time equally between them, although scholars differed over whether dividing his time equally was obligatory or recommended for him. They also differed over whether equity in maintenance is obligatory or recommended. The view that it is obligatory is stronger and more consistent with the Quran and Sunnah.

Such equity is enjoined as long as she remains his wife. If he wishes to divorce either wife, he may do so. But if he and the wife he wishes to divorce agree that she will remain with him without an allotted share of his time, and she willingly accepts this, that is permissible. As Allah Most High says:

“If a woman fears ill-treatment or neglect from her husband, there is no blame upon them if they reach a settlement between themselves, for settlement is best.”

In the Sahīh, ‘Ā’ishah said:

“This verse was revealed concerning a woman who has been married to a man for a long time, and he wishes to divorce her. She says, ‘Do not divorce me; keep me as your wife, and I release you from any obligation concerning my day.’ Then this verse was revealed.”

“The Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—had intended to divorce Sawdah, but she gave her day to ‘Ā’ishah, so he kept her as his wife without allotting her a share of his time.”

Something similar also happened to Rāfi‘ ibn Khadīj, and it is said that the verse was revealed concerning him.

He—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked:

What about a man who has two wives and favours one over the other in maintenance and all other rights, to the point that he has abandoned the other? What must he do?

---

## Page 15873

He replied:

He must treat both wives equitably and may not favour either of them in the division of his time, for the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“Whoever has two wives and inclines towards one more than the other will come on the Day of Resurrection with one side of his body leaning.”

If he does not treat them equitably, he must either maintain the marriage on fair terms or end it with kindness. And Allah knows best.

He—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked:

If a man abstains from intercourse with his wife for a month or two, is he sinful? Can the husband be required to have intercourse with her?

He replied:

A man must have intercourse with his wife in a reasonable manner. This is among her most binding rights over him, greater even than her right to food. As for “obligatory intercourse,” some say that it is obligatory once every four months. Others say that its frequency should be according to her need and his ability, just as he provides her with food according to her need and his means. This is the more correct of the two views. And Allah knows best.

He—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked: (*)

What about a woman who inserts a medicine into herself at the time of intercourse to prevent semen from entering the passages through which conception takes place? Is this permissible and lawful? If the medicine remains inside her after intercourse and does not come out, may she pray and fast after performing the ritual bath?

> **Footnotes:**
> (*) Shaykh Nāsir ibn Hamad al-Fahd stated (p. 234):
> “I previously cited this fatwa at 21/297; a note concerning it was also provided there.”

---

## Page 15874

He replied:

Her fasting and prayer are valid even if that medicine remains inside her. As for whether this practice is permissible, the scholars differ; the more cautious course is to refrain from it. And Allah knows best.

He—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked:

If a man looks at and touches every part of his wife’s body, including her private parts, does he incur any blame?

He replied:

It is not forbidden for a man to look at or touch any part of his wife’s body. However, looking at her private parts is disliked. Another view is that it is not disliked, while a further view is that it is disliked only during intercourse.

He—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked:

A divorced woman who is breastfeeding has contracted to breastfeed for payment. Her waiting period then ends, and she remarries. May the person who hired her prevent her from joining her husband, for fear that she will become pregnant by him and her milk supply for the child will diminish?

He replied:

Mere uncertainty is not grounds for denying the husband his right to intercourse, especially since it is authentically reported that the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“I had indeed considered forbidding

---

## Page 15875

that, but then I recalled that the Persians and Romans do so without harming their children.” He—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—was thus informed that they did this without harming the children, and he did not forbid it. Accordingly, the husband may not be denied his right, provided that exercising it does not infringe the prior entitlement established by the nursing contract.

He—may Allah Most High have mercy on him—was asked:

If a father cannot afford nursing fees, and the mother refuses to breastfeed unless she is paid, may he have another woman breastfeed the child?

He replied:

Yes, because he is not obliged to do what he cannot afford.

Shaykh al-Islām—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked:

A man is plagued by three: his wife, a cat, and ants. His wife breastfeeds a child who is not her own, thereby disrupting his life and marital relations; the cat eats the chicks; and the ants crawl into the food. Is it permissible for them to burn their dwellings with fire, or not? May they kill the cat? May he prevent his wife from breastfeeding?

He replied:

A wife may not breastfeed a child other than her own without her husband’s permission.

If the cat attacks his property, he may repel its attack, even by killing it. He may also remove it to a distant place. If its harm cannot be prevented except by killing it, it may be killed.

As for the ants, their harm should be prevented by means other than burning. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 15876

Chapter on Marital Disobedience

Shaykh al-Islām—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked:

A man has a wife who fasts during the day and prays at night. Whenever he calls her to bed, she refuses, giving precedence to night prayer and daytime fasting over obedience to her husband. Is this permissible?

He replied:

This is not lawful for her, by the agreement of the Muslims. Rather, she must obey him when he calls her to bed; this is a binding obligation upon her. Night prayer and daytime fasting, however, are voluntary acts. How, then, can a believing woman give precedence to a voluntary act over an obligatory one? The Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—even said, in the hadith narrated by al-Bukhārī and Muslim from Abū Hurayrah:

“It is not lawful for a woman to fast while her husband is present except with his permission, nor may she admit anyone into his house without his permission.”

Abū Dāwūd, Ibn Mājah and others also narrated it, with the wording:

“A woman must not fast a day outside Ramadan while her husband is present except with his permission.”

Thus, if the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—has forbidden a woman to observe a voluntary fast while her husband is present without his permission, since by fasting she withholds some of what she owes him:

---

## Page 15877

then what of her when he calls her and she refuses? In the two Sahīh collections, the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“When a man calls his wife to his bed and she refuses, the angels curse her until morning.”

Another wording states:

“The One in heaven is displeased with her until morning.”

Allah Most High has said:

“So righteous women are devoutly obedient, guarding in their husbands’ absence what Allah has commanded them to guard.”

A righteous woman is thus one who is “devoutly obedient,” meaning steadfast in obeying her husband. Whenever she refuses his call to bed, she is sinful and guilty of marital disobedience. This makes it permissible for him to strike her, as Allah Most High has said:

“As for those whose disobedience you fear, admonish them, forsake them in bed, and strike them. But if they obey you, seek no means against them.”

After the rights of Allah and His Messenger, no right is more binding upon a woman than her husband’s. Indeed, the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“If I were to command anyone to prostrate to another, I would command a woman to prostrate to her husband, so great is his right over her.”

It is also reported of him—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—that women said to him:

“Men strive in battle, give charity, and perform other deeds, while we do not do those things.” He replied, “The good conduct of any one of you is equal to that.”

This means that when a woman treats her husband well, this brings her Allah’s pleasure and honour, without her having to undertake the deeds specific to men. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 15878

He—may Allah Most High have mercy on him—was asked:

A man swore an oath to his wife, saying, “I will surely shun you if you do not pray.” She refused to pray and did not do so, and he stayed away from her bed. Is she entitled to maintenance from her husband or not? What is required of her if she abandons prayer?

He replied:

Praise be to Allah. If she refuses to pray, she is to be called upon to repent. If she repents, so be it; otherwise, she is to be put to death. Shunning someone for abandoning prayer is among the righteous deeds that Allah and His Messenger love. She has no right to maintenance if she will consent to marital intimacy with him only while continuing to abandon prayer. And Allah knows best.

He—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked:

Concerning a man whose wife does not pray: must he command her to pray? If she does not do so, must he separate from her or not?

He replied:

Yes, he must command her to pray; this is obligatory upon him. Indeed, he must command everyone whom he is able to command to do so, if no one else undertakes this duty. For He has said:

---

## Page 15879

Most High:

“Enjoin prayer upon your family and be steadfast in it,” and the remainder of the verse.

And He, Most High, said:

“O you who believe, protect yourselves and your families from a Fire whose fuel is people and stones,” and the remainder of the verse.

And he—peace and blessings be upon him—said:

“Teach them and discipline them.”

Alongside commanding her, he should encourage her by making prayer appealing to her, just as he encourages her to do what he needs from her. If she persists in abandoning prayer, he must divorce her; this is obligatory according to the correct view. By the consensus of the Muslims, anyone who abandons prayer deserves punishment until he prays. Indeed, if he does not pray, he is to be put to death. Whether he is put to death as an unbeliever and apostate is a matter on which there are two well-known opinions. And Allah knows best.

He—may Allah Most High have mercy on him—was asked:

Concerning His words, Most High:

“As for those women whose defiance you fear, admonish them, forsake them in bed, and strike them,”

and His words, Most High:

“When you are told to rise, then rise,”

through to His words, Most High:

“And Allah is fully aware of what you do.”

Would our shaykh explain to us the difference between “defiance” in the first passage and “rising” in the second?

He replied:

Praise be to Allah, Lord of all worlds. “Defiance” in His words, Most High,

“Whose defiance you fear, admonish them and forsake them in bed,”

means that a woman turns away from her husband and becomes averse to him, such that she does not obey him when he calls her to bed, or leaves his home without his permission, or does similar things involving refusal to obey him in matters in which she owes him obedience.

---

## Page 15880

As for nushūz (“rising”) in His words, Most High:

“When you are told to rise, then rise,”

it means getting up, standing, and rising. The basic sense of this root is elevation and roughness. From it comes al-nashr, meaning an elevated, rugged tract of land. Likewise, His words, Most High:

“And look at the bones—how We raise them,”

mean that We raise them one to another. Those who read nunshizuhā understand it to mean, “We bring them to life.” A disobedient woman is therefore called nāshiz (“defiant”) because of her harshness and her holding herself above obedience to her husband. Getting up is called nushūz because a seated person rises from the ground. And Allah knows best.

He—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked:

Concerning a man whose wife is defiant and refuses him intimacy: does her entitlement to maintenance and clothing lapse, and what is required of her?

He replied:

Praise be to Allah. Her entitlement to maintenance and clothing lapses if she refuses him intimacy, and he may strike her if she persists in her defiance. It is not lawful for her to refuse him this when he requests it; rather, she is disobeying Allah and His Messenger. It is related in the Sahīh:

“When a man calls his wife to his bed and she refuses him, the One above heaven is displeased with her until morning.”

---

## Page 15881

Shaykh al-Islām—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked:

Concerning a man whose wife has defiantly remained apart from him in her father’s house for eight months, during which he has had no marital relations with her?

He replied:

If she is defiant toward him, she is not entitled to maintenance. He may strike her if she is defiant, harms him, or acts aggressively toward him.

He—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked:

What is required of a husband if his wife refuses him intimacy when he requests it?

He replied:

Praise be to Allah. It is not lawful for her to be defiant toward him or to refuse him intimacy. Rather, if she refuses him and persists in doing so, he may strike her, but not severely, and she is not entitled to maintenance or an allotted share of his nights.

He was asked:

Concerning a man who married a woman and consummated the marriage, and who continues to provide her with maintenance although she is defiant. Her father then took her away and travelled with her without her husband’s permission. What is required of them both?

---

## Page 15882

He replied:

Praise be to Allah. If he travelled with her without her husband’s permission, he is subject to discretionary punishment for doing so. The wife is likewise subject to discretionary punishment if she could have remained behind. She is not entitled to maintenance from the time she departed. And Allah knows best.

He—may Allah Most High have mercy on him—was asked: (*)

Concerning a man who married a woman eleven years ago. She had lived harmoniously with him, but now refuses to continue marital life with him and is defiant toward him. What is required of her?

He replied:

It is not lawful for her to be defiant toward him or to refuse him intimacy. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“No man calls his wife to his bed and she refuses him without the One who is above the heaven being angry with her until morning.”

If she persists in her defiance, he may strike her. If a woman does not fulfil her obligations toward her husband, he is not obliged to divorce her and give her the dower. Rather, she must secure her release from him by offering her dower in return for his separation from her, just as “the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, instructed, concerning the wife of Thābit ibn Qays ibn Shammās, that her dower be handed over and that he separate from her.” If he cannot afford to pay the dower, it is not permissible to demand payment from him, according to the consensus of the Muslims.

He—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked:

Concerning a man who married a woman but derives no benefit from his marriage to her. She does not comply with him in any matter, yet demands maintenance and clothing from him and has made his affairs difficult. Is she entitled to maintenance and clothing from him?

> **Footnotes:**
> (*) Shaykh Nāsir ibn Hamad al-Fahd states (p. 234):
> This fatwa is repeated at 34/76.

---

## Page 15883

He replied:

If she refuses him marital intimacy or leaves his home without his permission, she is not entitled to maintenance or clothing. Likewise, if he asks her to travel with him and she does not do so, she is not entitled to maintenance or clothing. Whenever she is defiant, disobeying him in matters in which she owes him obedience, he is not obliged to provide her with maintenance or clothing.

He—may Allah Most High have mercy on him—was asked:

Concerning a married woman who has relatives whom she visits: whenever she wishes to visit them, she takes the bedding and stays with them for ten days or more. She is now close to giving birth. If she gives birth at their home, she will not be able to return to her own home for several days, leaving her husband cold. Is it permissible for them to let her give birth at their home?

He replied:

It is not lawful for a wife to leave her home without her husband’s permission, nor is it lawful for anyone to take her into their home and keep her away from her husband, whether because she is a wet nurse, a midwife, or engaged in some other occupation. If she leaves her husband’s home without his permission, she is defiant, disobedient to Allah and His Messenger, and deserving of punishment.

---

## Page 15884

Chapter on Khulʿ (Marital Dissolution in Return for Compensation)

The shaykh—may Allah Most High have mercy on him—was asked:

What is the khulʿ prescribed in the Quran and Sunnah?

He replied:

The khulʿ prescribed in the Quran and Sunnah is when a woman dislikes her husband and wishes to leave him, so she gives him the dowry, or part of it, to ransom herself, just as a captive is ransomed. But when both spouses wish to remain with one another, such khulʿ is an innovation introduced into Islam.

He—may Allah have mercy on him—also said: If she dislikes him and chooses to leave him, she ransoms herself from him by returning the dowry she received and releasing him from what he still owes her. He then grants her khulʿ, as prescribed in the Quran and Sunnah and agreed upon by the imams. Allah knows best.

He—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked:

Concerning a woman who disliked her husband and sought khulʿ from him, saying to him, “If you do not release me, I will kill myself.” The guardian then compelled him to separate from her, and she married another man. The first husband has now sought her return, saying that he separated from her under compulsion, while she wishes to remain only with the second husband?

---

## Page 15885

He replied:

If the first husband was rightfully compelled to separate from her—for example, because he was neglecting his duties towards her or unjustly harming her by word or deed—then the separation was valid, the second marriage is valid, and she is the second man’s wife. But if he was compelled through beating or imprisonment to separate from her while treating her well, the separation did not take effect. Rather, if she dislikes him while he treats her well, he should be asked to separate from her without being compelled to do so. If he agrees, well and good; otherwise, the woman should be instructed to bear patiently with him, provided there are no grounds permitting annulment of the marriage.

He—may Allah Most High have mercy on him—was asked:

Concerning a man who accused his wife of indecency, although he had seen nothing in her conduct that Islamic law condemns, except that [he] (1) claimed to have sent her to a wedding and then secretly checked on her but did not find her there. She denied this. He then went to her guardians and told them what had happened. They summoned her to meet her husband concerning what had been mentioned, but she refused for fear of being beaten and went to her maternal uncle’s house. The husband subsequently used this as grounds for depriving her of her entitlement, claiming that she had left without his permission. Does this invalidate her entitlement? Does her denial of his allegation warrant censure under Islamic law?

He replied:

Allah Most High says:

“O you who believe, it is not lawful for you to inherit women against their will. Nor should you make life difficult for them in order to take back some of what you have given them, unless they commit a clear indecency.”

Thus, it is not lawful for a man to constrain his wife by restricting her and making life difficult for her

> **Footnotes:**
> (1) The text in square brackets is absent from the printed edition, and I have not found it in the book Safeguarding Majmū‘ al-Fatāwā against Omissions and Scribal Errors.
> Usāma ibn al-Zahrā’ — coordinator of the book for the al-Shāmila Encyclopaedia.

---

## Page 15886

until she gives him part of her dowry, nor may he beat her for that purpose. But if she commits a clear indecency, he may constrain her so that she pays compensation to secure her release from the marriage; he may also beat her. This concerns the matter as it stands between the man and Allah. As for “the woman’s family,” they should ascertain who is in the right and support that person in securing their rights. If it becomes clear to them that she is the one who has transgressed Allah’s limits and wronged her husband in his marriage bed, then she is acting wrongfully and transgressing, and should pay compensation to secure her release from him.

If he says that he sent her to a wedding but she did not go there, he should ask where she went. If he states that she went to people among whom there is no cause for suspicion, and those people confirm her account, or say, “She did not come to us,” while she did not go to the wedding either, then this is cause for suspicion and strengthens the husband’s claim.

As for the “bridal trousseau” she brought from her father’s house, he must return it to her in all circumstances. If they reach a settlement, reconciliation is best. Whenever the woman repents, her husband may retain her as his wife, and there is no blame in that, for one who repents of a sin is like one who has no sin. If they cannot agree on her return to him, she should release him from his obligation to pay the dowry, and the husband should grant her a divorce in exchange. Such a divorce is permissible according to the Book of Allah and the Sunnah of His Messenger, as Allah Most High says:

“But if you fear that they will not keep within the limits of Allah, then there is no blame upon either of them concerning that by which she ransoms herself.”

And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 15887

Shaykh al-Islām—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked:

An adult woman who was no longer a virgin had no marriage guardian other than the judge, who married her off because she had no other guardians. Her husband subsequently divorced her in exchange for compensation, and she released him from his obligation to pay the dowry without the judge’s permission. Are the divorce and the release valid?

He replied:

If she is legally competent to dispose of her property without compensation, her divorce in exchange for compensation and her release of him from the obligation are valid without the judge’s permission.

He—may Allah have mercy on him—was also asked:

A woman’s husband said to her, “If you release me from my obligation, you are divorced.” She released him, and she was not legally barred from managing her property, nor did she have a father or brother. She then claimed that she was financially imprudent in order to invalidate the release.

He replied:

The release is not invalidated merely by her claim. Even if evidence established that she was financially imprudent, but she had not been legally barred from managing her property, that would not invalidate the release—even if she was managing her own affairs. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 15888

He—may Allah Most High have mercy on him—was asked:

A woman released her husband from his obligation to pay her entire dowry. Afterwards, the husband had witnesses attest that he had divorced his wife in exchange for that release, although the release had already taken place. Is the divorce valid? If it takes effect, is it revocable or not?

He replied:

If they had agreed that she would give up her dowry and release him from his obligation in exchange for his divorcing her, and she then released him and he divorced her, that constitutes an irrevocable divorce. The same applies if he said to her, “Release me from my obligation, and I will divorce you,” or, “If you release me from my obligation, I will divorce you,” or used similar expressions, whether those of the learned or ordinary people, which convey that he requested the release in exchange for divorcing her.

If, however, she released him from his obligation independently of any divorce, and he subsequently divorced her, the divorce is revocable. But may she retract the release, where doing so is possible, on the grounds that such a release is ordinarily granted only so that he will keep her as his wife, or out of fear that he will divorce her, take another wife, or the like? There are two opinions on this, both reported from Ahmad.

If, however, she willingly granted the release unconditionally—that is, she initiated it herself, without anything on his part prompting it and without receiving anything in return—then she unquestionably has no right to retract it. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 15889

He—may Allah Most High have mercy on him—was asked about a man who said to his wife:

“This son of your husband is not to enter any house of mine, for he is my son; I raised him.”

When he complained about him to his father, the latter asked the husband, “If your wife releases you from your obligations, will you divorce her?”

He replied, “Yes.”

He then brought her, and the husband said to her, “If you release me from my obligations under your document and the deed you hold against me, then you are divorced?”

She said, “Yes.”

They parted, and the husband went upstairs to his neighbours’ home and said, “She is divorced three times.” He then went down to the witnesses, who asked him, “How many times was she divorced?”

He replied, “Three,” referring to what he had pronounced. Does the threefold divorce take effect against him?

He replied:

Praise be to Allah. If, as the context indicates, her release of him from his obligations was not unconditional, but rather conditional upon his divorcing her, then she became irrevocably divorced from him, and no subsequent divorce took effect upon her. A condition agreed before a contract is like one stipulated when it is concluded, and a condition understood through custom is like one expressly stated.

The question put to him by the man acting on her behalf—“If your wife comes and releases you from your obligations, will you divorce her?”—and his reply amount to a stipulation that he divorce her if she releases him. His subsequently bringing her, his saying, “You, if you release me,” and her replying, “Yes,” are to be understood on that basis: if she releases him, he will divorce her.

Thus, if she said, “I have released him,” but he refused to divorce her, the release would not be valid. In customary usage, this constitutes an offer and acceptance, in view of the preceding conditions and the circumstances. Its meaning is: “I release you from your obligations on condition that you divorce me.”

---

## Page 15890

Shaykh al-Islām—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked:

A man pronounced one revocable divorce upon his wife. When he appeared before the witnesses, one of them told him, “Say: ‘I divorced her in return for a dirham.’” He said this, whereupon they told him, “She now has control over her own affairs and cannot return to you without her consent.” If he is prevented from taking her back, does he lose his right despite having been misled in this way, or not?

He replied:

Praise be to Allah. If he had pronounced one revocable divorce upon her, and a witness then prompted him to say that he had divorced her in return for a dirham, and he said this believing that he was acknowledging the first divorce rather than effecting another, then no divorce takes effect beyond the first, which remains revocable, not irrevocable.

If it is alleged that he made the second statement to effect a second divorce, but he says, “I said it only to acknowledge the first divorce,” and he does not know that divorce in return for compensation makes the divorce irrevocable, then his account is to be accepted upon his oath—especially since the surrounding circumstances support it. For customary practice is that, when a man divorces his wife and then appears before witnesses, he does so only for them to attest to the divorce that has already taken place.

---

## Page 15891

He was also asked—may Allah Most High have mercy on him—about khulʿ (marital dissolution in return for compensation):

“Is it a divorce that counts as one of the three? And is it a condition that neither the wording nor the intention of divorce be involved?”

He replied—may Allah Most High have mercy on him:

There is a well-known disagreement on this issue among the early and later scholars. The apparent position of Imam Ahmad and his followers is that it constitutes an irrevocable separation and an annulment of the marriage, and does not count as one of the three divorces. Thus, even if a man were to conclude khulʿ with his wife ten times, he could marry her again under a new marriage contract without her first marrying another husband. This is also one of al-Shāfiʿī’s two positions. Some of his followers adopted it and argued in its support; others argued in its support without adopting it.

This is the position of the majority of the hadith jurists, such as Ishāq ibn Rāhawayh, Abū Thawr, Dāwūd, Ibn al-Mundhir and Ibn Khuzaymah. It is authentically established from Ibn ʿAbbās and his followers, such as Tāwūs and ʿIkrimah.

The second position is that it constitutes an irrevocable divorce that counts as one of the three. This is the view of many of the early scholars, and is the position of Abū Hanīfah and Mālik, as well as al-Shāfiʿī’s other position—which is said to be his later position. It is also the other position reported from Ahmad.

This view is attributed to ʿUmar, ʿUthmān, ʿAlī and Ibn Masʿūd. However, Ahmad and other leading hadith scholars, such as Ibn al-Mundhir, Ibn Khuzaymah, al-Bayhaqī and others, judged the reports from these Companions to be weak. They authenticated only Ibn ʿAbbās’s statement that it is an annulment, not a divorce.

As for al-Shāfiʿī and others, they said: “We do not know the standing of the person who narrated this from ʿUthmān: is he trustworthy or not?” Thus, they did not authenticate what was reported from the Companions; rather, they acknowledged that they did not know whether it was authentic.

---

## Page 15892

And I know of no scholar versed in transmitted reports who has authenticated the reports from the Companions that khulʿ constitutes an irrevocable divorce counting as one of the three. Rather, the strongest report they have on this matter is the one attributed to ʿUthmān. Yet it has been transmitted from ʿUthmān through an authentic chain that he instructed a woman who had obtained khulʿ to establish that she was not pregnant by observing one menstrual period. He told her: “You are not required to observe a waiting period.”

This implies that, in his view, it constitutes an irrevocable separation, not a divorce. For divorce after consummation requires a waiting period of three periods, according to the explicit text of the Quran and the agreement of the Muslims. Khulʿ is different: the Sunnah and reports from the Companions establish that its waiting period consists of ascertaining that the woman is not pregnant by observing one menstrual period. This is the position of Ishāq, Ibn al-Mundhir and others, and one of the two positions reported from Ahmad.

Ibn ʿAbbās returned a woman to her husband after two divorces and one khulʿ, without her first marrying another husband. When Ibn al-Zubayr appointed Ibrāhīm ibn Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqās governor of Yemen, Ibrāhīm asked Ibn ʿAbbās about this issue, saying: “Most divorces among the people of Yemen take the form of release in return for compensation.” Ibn ʿAbbās replied that release in return for compensation is not divorce; rather, people had mistakenly called it that.

Ibn ʿAbbās cited as evidence the words of Allah Most High:

“Divorce is twice; then either retain her honorably or release her with kindness. It is not lawful for you to take back anything you have given them, unless both fear that they cannot keep within the limits set by Allah. If you fear that they cannot keep within the limits set by Allah, there is no blame on either of them concerning what she gives to secure her release. These are the limits set by Allah, so do not transgress them. Whoever transgresses the limits set by Allah—it is they who are the wrongdoers.”

“Then if he divorces her, she is not lawful for him afterward until she marries another husband.”

Ibn ʿAbbās said: Allah Most High mentioned release in return for compensation after divorce twice, then said:

“Then if he divorces her, she is not lawful for him afterward until she marries another husband.”

This applies specifically to cases involving release in return for compensation, and generally to other cases. Thus, if release in return for compensation were itself a divorce, there would be four divorces. Ahmad, according to the well-known position reported from him, and the scholars mentioned earlier followed Ibn ʿAbbās.

---

## Page 15893

These scholars differed over whether a woman who has obtained khulʿ must observe a waiting period of three periods or establish that she is not pregnant by observing one menstrual period. There are two views, both reported from Ahmad. According to one, she establishes that she is not pregnant by observing one menstrual period. This is the view of ʿUthmān, Ibn ʿAbbās, and Ibn ʿUmar according to the later of the two reports from him. It was also held by several of the early generations and is the position of Ishāq, Ibn al-Mundhir, and others. Reports to this effect are also related from the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, in the Sunan collections through several good chains of transmission, which have been detailed elsewhere.

This is among the evidence cited by those who hold that khulʿ does not count as one of the three divorces. They argue that, if it did, the explicit text of the Quran would require a waiting period of three periods. They also cite this as evidence of the weakness of the report attributing to ʿUthmān the view that it constitutes one irrevocable divorce. For it is established from him through an acceptable chain of transmission that he required the woman to establish that she was not pregnant by observing one menstrual period. Had she been divorced, she would have been required to wait for three periods.

If it is argued, “Rather, ʿUthmān regarded her as divorced but required her to establish that she was not pregnant by observing one menstrual period,” then this is a view that no scholar has held. It is therefore more appropriate to follow ʿUthmān according to the established report from him—a position shared by Ibn ʿAbbās and supported by the Quran and Sunnah—than to follow a report whose narrator is unknown: the report of Jumhān al-Aslamī from him stating that he regarded it as one irrevocable divorce.
